Its manager, a broad woman with a pencil in her hair, refused to discuss patients in a reception area where three customers could hear her.","Mara liked the refusal, although it was inconvenient.","\"We don't want a diagnosis,\" Noor said. \"We want to preserve a journey record and find out which organization instructed the vehicle.\"","\"Then put that in writing. The distinction will matter to my insurer.\"","The manager's name was Petra Cole. Her office contained a model ambulance, a bowl of sour sweets, and an inventory board crowded with coloured pins. Beyond the window, a mechanic lay beneath a shuttle bus. A driver was trying to explain why a missing wing mirror should qualify as an act of God.","\"It was a bollard,\" Petra told him. \"God has enough complaints.\"","Cassian showed her the independent missing-person inquiry reference Holt had helped obtain. It did not grant unlimited access to private records. It did give Petra a route to share relevant transport information with the investigator handling the inquiry. She called that office herself before agreeing to preserve anything.","Mara watched the procedure with mixed gratitude and frustration. A barrier that delayed them could also prevent someone like Dacre from finding a resident who had chosen to leave. It was tempting to prefer privacy only while it protected one's own question.","While they waited, Rowan called. He had identified the purchasing agent through a public company register. The company arranged transport and equipment payments for several care providers; its name alone could not identify which one had ordered Reed's journey.","\"I could send you an impressive diagram,\" he said. \"It would mostly prove that somebody likes subsidiaries.\"","\"Send the sources instead,\" Mara replied.","\"How cruel. I had already chosen colours.\"","Behind the joke, his voice had become careful. The agent sometimes dealt with a finance company associated with the Ash family. Rowan offered that fact before she asked, then specified its limits: he had seen a public filing, not the private account for this transfer.","\"If there's a family connection, I don't want you finding it after I've been charming for three days,\" he said.","Mara looked towards Cassian. He had heard enough to understand and said nothing that would make Rowan's disclosure easier to dismiss as theatre.","\"Thank you,\" she said. \"We will check it like the rest.\"","\"An alarming prospect. I recommend my better side.\"","Petra returned with two sheets.","\"K-17 is an account code,\" she said. \"Not a vehicle. It covers a number of clients served through the same purchasing agent.\"","Their neat theory acquired an inconvenient width.","\"Could the destination be blank?\" Cassian asked.","\"On the customer's copy, yes. Some addresses are sent directly to the driver. But the journey can't be paid without a completion record.\"","\"Can someone erase that record?\"","\"Someone can attempt to. We keep backups. We also pay a great deal for them. I'd be disappointed if secrecy turned out to be their only feature.\"","She checked the transfer date. An ambulance had collected a male passenger from Kingfisher at nine ten. A second attendant had accompanied him. The route ended at a medical receiving centre whose licence had expired six weeks earlier.","\"The address could have been used for an administrative handover,\" Noor said. \"It doesn't establish that he was treated there.\"","\"No,\" Petra agreed. \"But it should have raised a question.\"","Her manager's manner fell away. She looked at the completion signature for a long time.","\"My driver's name is on this. I don't recognise his hand.\"","Cassian asked her to retain the original file. Petra printed another copy, marked the time, and called the driver.","He arrived carrying a packed lunch and a tiny child's cardigan. His daughter had left it in his cab. Mara felt an absurd tenderness towards the garment; it made the man more solid than the box beside his name.","He was called Ben. He read the document, then frowned.","\"I drove to Kingfisher,\" he said. \"But not to that centre.\"","\"Where did you go?\"","\"The south tram depot. The passenger asked to get out. The attendant said it was planned.\"","\"Was he in distress?\" Noor asked.","\"He was quiet. I can't tell you what that meant. He said thank you.\"","\"And the attendant?\"","\"Stayed with him on the platform. I was told another vehicle would collect them.\"","He looked at Petra. \"I should have called you.\"","\"Yes,\" she said. \"We'll deal with that separately. First, we need your actual route.\"","She was neither indulgent nor cruel. Mara wondered how often useful leadership consisted of refusing to make one error explain an entire person.","The vehicle's location record supported Ben's account. It showed a fourteen-minute stop at the depot, then a return to the garage. It did not show what happened after Reed got out. The discrepancy weakened the reliability of the completion sheet; it did not establish who had changed it.","Ben handed Cassian a small object wrapped in a napkin.","\"He left this in the seat. I meant to put it in lost property.\"","Inside lay a broken chess knight. A bird had been glued to its head.","Mara took a breath. At last, something was small enough to hold and definite enough to recognise.","Leda met them at the depot. She had left rehearsal in her work clothes, a paint stain on one shoulder. When Mara opened the napkin, she covered her mouth.","\"I made it,\" she said. \"That ugly little thing.\"","\"It's an eagle,\" Cassian said.","\"It is a pigeon. Dad insisted on promotion.\"","She laughed, then began to cry. Noor stood with her while Mara and Cassian checked the depot's public area. They found a closed ticket office, a vending machine selling bitter coffee, and an advertisement for a lottery promising a new life by Friday.","\"Convenience has improved,\" Mara said, looking at the bright machine. \"Fulfilment remains unpredictable.\"","Cassian brought her coffee anyway. It was worse than expected.","\"A generous gesture,\" she told him, \"with adverse empirical results.\"","\"I shall amend my method.\"","She liked that they could still tease each other here. She was ashamed of liking it while Leda wept, until Noor caught her eye and smiled slightly, as though sadness did not require every other feeling to leave the room.","The depot attendant remembered a man with a folder. He had sat under the old departure board, watched the road for a while, and asked whether the Lantern Theatre still offered standing-room tickets.","\"Did he leave with anyone?\" Cassian asked.","\"A woman in a green coat came over. They talked. Then he walked towards the river. I can't say they went together. I turned away to answer a question.\"","A camera might settle part of that uncertainty. Its owner would not provide footage directly to them, but agreed to preserve it for the inquiry. Cassian gave the time range and obtained a written reference.","Mara had begun to understand how much of an investigation was an exercise in leaving doors marked for people who could lawfully open them later.","Leda joined them. She held the chess piece with both hands.","\"He used to leave things for me,\" she said. \"When he worked late. Little puzzles. I would hunt for a note in his coat, then find another one in the bread tin.\"","\"Could this be intentional?\" Mara asked.","\"Possibly. Or he just left it. I don't want to pretend I know.\"","The honesty cost her something. Mara squeezed her shoulder after asking with a glance, and Leda leaned into the brief contact.","\"What did he say about the theatre?\" she asked.","The attendant repeated his account. Leda looked towards the river.","\"The old play,\" she said. \"The exile who won't surrender his name. There's a scene with a bird in a cage. Dad loved it. He said the author was trying to put a quart into a pint pot: a whole city into one person.\"","\"Does the bird escape?\" Cassian asked.","\"The cage is opened. The play never tells you whether it leaves.\"","They went to the theatre after lunch. June had persuaded the stage manager to let them examine the props stored from the old production. He required an inventory and insisted that they move nothing without him present.","\"Customer loyalty,\" he said, \"doesn't extend to people who steal my scenery.\"","\"We'll try to deserve your trust,\" Mara said.","The birdcage stood in a dusty alcove. It was empty. Beneath it, someone had pasted a paper feather to the shelf.","Leda recognised her father's handwriting on the back.","No applause. Wait until the house is quiet.","Mara looked towards the stage, where the evening rehearsal had begun. The actors were laughing over an entrance that kept going wrong. A cascade of light fell across the empty seats.","She wondered whether Reed had come here for refuge, or merely to leave one more thing behind before he add to Ben’s account?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. They identify the person who altered the completion signature.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. They show that the receiving centre accepted Reed that morning.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"8\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"10\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v01c11","title":"When the House Was Quiet","volume":"卷一｜假婚约与失踪证人","anchor":"chapter-0011","paragraphs":["They waited through a rehearsal that involved a false marriage, three misplaced letters, and a general who kept losing his moustache. The theatre's young instrumentalist played the same phrase until Mara could anticipate the mistake before he made it. Then, unexpectedly, he played it correctly, and the whole room seemed to breathe.","Leda watched from the back row. The chess eagle rested in her palm.","\"Dad would have liked that,\" she said. \"The improvement nobody applauded because they were looking somewhere else.\"","Mara understood. Restoration produced a similar kind of invisibility. If the work succeeded, people admired the object and forgot that it had ever needed a hand.","Cassian sat beside her, his shoulder close but not touching. In addition to the packet and the birdcage, Reed might have left nothing. Mara tried to prepare herself for that possibility without disguising it as wisdom.","June joined them carrying a bottle of water and an old programme. She had found a cast photograph in which Reed stood beside the scenery. The note beneath it identified him as a volunteer carpenter.","\"He built the cage's platform,\" she said.","\"It was a bit of everything,\" Leda recalled. \"A stool, a table, a prison, a throne. The company was short of money.\"","The stage manager looked up from his keys. \"That condition has become a long-standing artistic tradition.\"","After the final actor left, he switched off the stage lamps. Only a low work light remained. The dark auditorium smelled of cloth, old wood, and the honey biscuits nobody had eaten at Kingfisher.","Mara went to the birdcage. Its platform was a plain box with four legs and a drawer that appeared decorative. She examined it methodically, first with her eyes, then with the flat of her uninjured hand.","\"May I?\" she asked Leda.","\"Please.\"","She found a raised edge beneath the front leg. A paper label had been painted over. The paint was old; the scratch beside it was new.","Cassian held the light while Mara loosened a screw. No hidden machinery sprang into operation. A small wooden cover came away, revealing an envelope held in place by a bent nail.","Leda did not reach for it immediately.","\"If that is a goodbye,\" she said, \"I don't know if I can read it.\"","Mara sat back on her heels. \"Then we can wait.\"","The stage manager quietly moved a stool beside the platform. Leda sat. For a while, nobody asked her to be brave.","At last she opened the envelope.","Inside was a letter addressed to her, a smaller sealed envelope without a name, and a photograph of an ordinary workbench. On its underside, nearly hidden by shadow, was the number seventeen.","Leda read the letter silently. Her face changed in small increments: recognition, anger, then a kind of grief Mara could not identify from outside.","\"He says he left the depot because he was afraid the attendant would send him back,\" she said. \"The woman in the green coat helped him find the theatre. He doesn't give her name.\"","\"Does he say where he went?\" June asked.","\"No. He says he didn't want anyone made to lie for him.\"","The letter could explain a choice; it could not guarantee that the choice had remained free after he made it. Mara watched Cassian absorb that limitation. He looked no less determined for refusing a comforting certainty.","Leda read another line aloud.","\"I remember the bench. I don't know what I remember about it. Sometimes that is worse than forgetting.\"","Noor asked whether there was a date. There was: the day of the transfer. The handwriting resembled Reed's earlier letters. That supported authorship, but the circumstances under which he wrote remained unknown.","\"He says to give the photograph and the sealed envelope to the person who opened the flower,\" Leda continued.","Mara's stomach tightened. \"Me.\"","\"Probably,\" Cassian said softly.","She nearly reproached him for the word. Then she realised that she had been about to transform a description into a guarantee. Mara had opened the flower. It did not follow that she was the only person who ever had.","June lowered her phone. \"Would you like a copy made?\"","Leda held the letter closer. \"Of the part about the bench. Not the rest.\"","\"Of course.\"","It would have been easy to ask what else her father had written. Mara felt the question form and let it go. Leda had come to find a person, not to donate every remaining piece of their relationship to an investigation.","The girl looked at her. \"He says he remembers a birthday wrong. He knows it is wrong because I told him about it afterward. He's afraid I will think the mistake means he didn't care.\"","\"You don't have to give us that,\" Mara said.","\"I wanted someone to know. That isn't the same as wanting everyone to know.\"","\"No.\"","Leda folded the private pages and put them inside her jacket. The action was small, but her shoulders settled afterward. She had chosen a boundary while still choosing to help. Mara wondered how many institutions would have called that hesitation instead of dignity.","Leda handed her the smaller envelope.","\"Open it,\" she said.","Mara did. It contained only a short note: Verify the repair record first. Penn knows the difference between a maker and a restorer. Then ask where this bench belongs.","The professor's name changed the shape of the room.","\"Was he at the theatre recently?\" Cassian asked the stage manager.","\"Two days ago. He came to look at the old programmes. He has been helping with the history exhibition.\"","June frowned. \"Could he have left this?\"","\"We cannot rule it out,\" Noor said.","Mara thought of the man at dinner, his kindness and his convenient expertise. She had been ready to regard him as a friend because he had spoken when she needed help. That had been useful. It had not been proof of allegiance.","\"We ask him,\" she said.","Professor Penn received them in a small flat above a bookshop. A violet lamp lit his desk, and a cat occupied a chair with the indifference of a senior civil servant. He looked at the photograph without surprise.","\"I wondered when you would find it.\"","Cassian remained standing. \"Did you leave the envelope?\"","\"No. Tomas asked me where he might safely leave it. I suggested the theatre because his daughter would understand the clue.\"","\"Why didn't you tell us?\"","The professor's confidence faltered. \"He asked me to wait. I thought he needed time.\"","\"He may have needed help,\" Mara said.","\"Yes.\"","It was an inadequate answer. His willingness to admit that did not make it adequate, but it prevented the conversation from becoming an argument about his intentions instead of Reed's danger.","Penn gave them a signed account. He had seen Reed briefly after the depot journey, outside the theatre. Reed had refused a cab and an offer to call Leda. He had asked Penn to bring the local repair register to Vey House, where, he believed, the woman with the flower would be invited.","\"He had seen the engagement announcement,\" Penn said. \"It gave him a way to reach you without going to the house.\"","Mara looked at Cassian. Their invented romance had become, without their knowledge, a public address.","\"Did he tell you where the bench was?\" Cassian asked.","\"He told me not to guess from memory. The register has a reference. I was due to collect it tomorrow.\"","\"We'll come with you,\" Mara said.","On the pavement outside, she felt suddenly exhausted. She had wanted a decisive message that would turn a missing person into someone waiting at the end of a journey. Instead, she had received another person who had tried to help, made a choice, and could not know whether it was right.","Cassian stood beside her beneath the bookshop awning.","\"I keep thinking I should have done more at Kingfisher,\" she said.","\"So do I.\"","\"You don't usually admit that.\"","\"I thought it might make the uncertainty harder for you.\"","\"It makes it less lonely.\"","He looked down at his hands. \"Then I will try to remember that.\"","She reached for his sleeve. A small gesture, easily withdrawn. He turned his arm enough to let her hold it comfortably.","\"We are terrible at pretending to be engaged,\" she said.","\"Our audience appears satisfied.\"","\"Our audience has low standards.\"","\"Your standards?\"","She considered the question. \"Still under review.\"","His smile was tired and unexpectedly warm.","They walked to the corner together. Mara did not tell him how much she wanted tomorrow to include him. It was enough, for now, that when the light changed, neither had to ask whether the other would cross."],"questions":[{"type":"attitude","stem":"How does Mara’s view of Penn change after she reads She dismisses his historical knowledge as irrelevant to the flower.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. She separates the value of his earlier help from proof that he can be fully trusted.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"9\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"11\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v01c12","title":"Room Seventeen","volume":"卷一｜假婚约与失踪证人","anchor":"chapter-0012","paragraphs":["The museum repair register had been kept in a cupboard that smelled of dust and furniture oil. Its registrar brought it to a table beneath a high window, then asked Professor Penn to stop touching the pages before she had found him a book cradle.","\"History survives affection poorly,\" she said.","Penn withdrew his hands with the shame of a boy caught near a cake.","Mara sat opposite him. Cassian stood by the window, where the morning light made the scar on his wrist almost disappear. Leda and Noor joined them, while June waited for permission to examine a separate body of correspondence. Nobody had been invited merely to watch.","The entry for the silver flower was forty-three years old. Its original maker had repaired automata for a small theatre company. The mechanism had later been damaged, then restored by Elena Vale. A second entry recorded alterations to the hinge, the replacement of a petal, and the addition of a removable compartment.","Mara read her mother's name several times.","It was not a revelation about where Elena was. It did not contradict the belief that she had died. It was an ordinary professional record, and its ordinariness moved Mara more than the extravagant catalogue had done.","\"She was here,\" she said.","The registrar looked up.","\"In the work,\" Mara explained. \"They left her in the record.\"","Cassian said nothing. He put a clean sheet of paper beside her so she could make notes without pressing on the page.","The repair mark beneath the flower matched the drawing in the register. Mara photographed both, with the registrar's approval. She also added a correction to her own account: Elena's mark established her restoration and later modifications, not the original manufacture of the whole mechanism.","\"That will weaken the dramatic version,\" Penn said.","\"Good,\" Mara replied. \"It was beginning to get ahead of the actual one.\"","The registrar smiled. Then she turned to the reference attached to the removable compartment.","Its fittings had been supplied through an adult conservation college. A later note recorded a transfer of benches, tools, and demonstration objects to a research wing. The register gave an institutional address, but the room list had been filed separately.","\"We need the original appendix,\" the registrar said. \"It may take a little time.\"","Mara looked at the photograph of the bench. Seventeen remained a dark shape beneath the drawer.","\"Then we wait,\" she said.","She was learning that waiting could be a task, provided it did not become an excuse for leaving someone else to disappear.","While the registrar searched, an update arrived from Holt. The preservation order on Mara's workshop had been narrowed. The former client's contract and Mrs. Cress's payment record showed that the cabinet had not simply been stolen. Mara could collect unrelated tools under supervision while the civil dispute continued.","\"You can work again,\" Cassian said.","\"I can work around the cabinet,\" Mara replied. \"He'll still owe me money.\"","\"A remarkably stable feature of the world.\"","She laughed, and felt some tight place inside her loosen.","The theatre received news too. The grant payment would proceed for the current repair stage. The foundation had not admitted that its review was improper; the trustees had simply been asked to identify the prior conditions in writing, and found that the additional financial test was not among them.","This was a limit imposed on one decision, not a transformation of the institution. It mattered nonetheless to the carpenter expecting his wages on Friday.","Leda read the message, then covered her face for a moment.","\"Dad would say it was a good day to be ordinary,\" she said.","Mara thought of the sealed workshop, the laundry, the transport board. Ordinary life looked much less ordinary when people could take it away.","They returned to the shop at midday. An inspector checked the list while Mara gathered her tools. She did not treat him as the author of every wrong done under the notice. He did not treat her as an accusation that happened to have hands.","When he reached the flower on the inventory sheet, she showed him the museum record. He made a note and left the ownership question open pending further documents. Cassian watched without using his name as a substitute for the missing proof.","Mrs. Cress had brought sandwiches. She looked at Mara's borrowed blouse, then at the silver feather pin.","\"You can keep the blouse until you've stopped running through walls.\"","\"A stringent condition.\"","\"I expect good clothes to enhance character. So far yours are merely surviving it.\"","Mara hugged her. The landlady pretended to find this an impolite interruption to lunch.","That afternoon, June delivered the corrected account of the dinner photograph. The full image did not identify the contents of Dacre's envelope, so she had not described it as a bribe. She had reported the agency's confirmed purchase of the cropped photograph and the invented name on Mara's invitation, with the limits of what could be established.","The article did not make everyone sympathetic. Several readers continued to insist that Mara looked like a thief. Other readers asked why a rehabilitation residence needed a publicity response to a request for a visit. The question had begun to move away from her appearance.","\"That is not complete vindication,\" June said.","\"I wouldn't recognise myself in complete vindication,\" Mara replied.","She had entered a warehouse without permission and taken objects from an auction. The fact that others had lied about her did not turn all her own choices into innocence. She would have to deal with those choices separately.
